This is way, way too many models most won't care about. Find a better way to list models, maybe only listing the 2 most recent per provider and the others available with an arrow to open all models, or click on a provider and show all of them.

## Summary

I've completed a thorough analysis of the LLM provider model listing and rendering code. Here are the findings:

### JavaScript Frontend Code (Model Listing & Rendering)

**Settings Page (Providers List):**
- File: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/succinct-pyroraptor/crates/web/src/assets/js/page-providers.js`
- Uses Preact + HTM for rendering
- Key component: `ProviderSection` (lines 194-286) renders individual provider groups
- Calls `groupProviderRows()` (lines 153-192) to organize models by provider
- **Sorting Logic** (lines 184-189):
  - First sorts by provider UI order metadata (`uiOrder`)
  - Models within each provider sorted by `createdAt` (descending - newest first), then alphabetically by display name
- Renders `providersConfiguredList` div (line 363) containing provider buttons and model cards
- Each model shows: display name, ID, badges (unsupported, chat-only, disabled), and `createdAt` formatted as `<time>` element with `data-epoch-ms` and `data-format="year-month"`

**Onboarding Page (Model Selection During Setup):**
- File: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/succinct-pyroraptor/crates/web/src/assets/js/onboarding-view.js`
- Uses Preact + HTM
- Function `toModelSelectorRow()` (line 1079) converts API model objects to display rows
- Function `showModelSelector()` (line 646) renders single-model selection with search
- Function `showMultiModelSelector()` (line 1124) renders preferred models selector for existing providers
- **Multi-model selector sorting** (lines 1188-1198):
  - Sorts by: selected status first, then by `createdAt` (descending), then alphabetically
  - Displays `createdAt` as `<time>` element with epoch milliseconds (line 1262)

**Model Selector in Chat:**
- File: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/succinct-pyroraptor/crates/web/src/assets/js/models.js`
- Uses signal-based state management with Preact Signals
- `modelStore` (line 49) is the source of truth for model data
- Function `fetchModels()` (lines 19-34) calls `models.list` RPC and populates the store
- Function `renderModelList()` (lines 100-128) renders the dropdown
- Filters by display name, provider, and model ID
- Shows divider between preferred and non-preferred models (line 122)

**Provider Setup (Vendor Flows):**
- File: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/succinct-pyroraptor/crates/web/src/assets/js/providers.js`
- Function `openModelSelectorForProvider()` (line 1073) opens multi-model selector
- Function `showMultiModelSelector()` (line 1124) handles preferred models UI
- Model cards include: name, ID, `createdAt` timestamp, probe status badges, tools support badge
- **Sorting in multi-select** (line 1189): `{ selected: 0/1, time: createdAt, name: displayName }`

### Model Data Structure & API

**Model Data Object (JSON):**
```json
{
  "id": "string",
  "provider": "string",
  "displayName": "string",
  "supportsTools": boolean,
  "preferred": boolean,
  "createdAt": number (milliseconds since epoch, optional),
  "unsupported": boolean,
  "unsupportedReason": string | null,
  "disabled": boolean
}
```

**RPC Endpoints:**
- `models.list` - Returns enabled models only (line 1728 in chat/src/lib.rs)
- `models.list_all` - Returns all models including disabled (line 1765 in chat/src/lib.rs)
- `providers.available` - Returns configured providers and their preferred models
- `models.detect_supported` - Background model probing with progress events
- `providers.save_models` - Save preferred models for a provider

### Rust Backend Code (Model Service)

**Files:**
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/succinct-pyroraptor/crates/chat/src/lib.rs` - Main model service implementation
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/succinct-pyroraptor/crates/gateway/src/methods/services.rs` - RPC method registration
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/succinct-pyroraptor/crates/providers/src/lib.rs` - ModelInfo struct definition

**ModelInfo Structure** (crates/providers/src/lib.rs, line 821):
```rust
pub struct ModelInfo {
    pub id: String,
    pub provider: String,
    pub display_name: String,
    pub created_at: Option<i64>,  // Unix timestamp in seconds
    // ... other fields
}
```

**Model Listing Response** (crates/chat/src/lib.rs, lines 1742-1762):
- `list()` method (line 1728) returns enabled models
- Each model serialized with: `id`, `provider`, `displayName`, `supportsTools`, `preferred`, `createdAt`, `unsupported`, `unsupportedReason`, `unsupportedProvider`, `unsupportedUpdatedAt`
- `createdAt` comes from the ModelInfo struct (line 1754: `"createdAt": m.created_at`)

**Model Prioritization** (lines 1688-1715):
- Models sorted using `prioritize_models()` method
- Respects configured priority order from provider setup
- Shows preferred models first in chat dropdown (divider at line 122 of models.js)

    17→var configuredModels = signal([]);

    19→var loading = signal(false);

    22→var detectError = signal("");
    23→var detectProgress = signal(null);
    24→var deletingProvider = signal("");

    26→
    27→function countUniqueProviders(models) {
    28→	return new Set(models.map((m) => m.provider)).size;
    29→}
    30→
    31→function progressFromPayload(payload) {
    32→	return {
    33→		total: payload?.total || 0,
    34→		checked: payload?.checked || 0,
    35→		supported: payload?.supported || 0,
    36→		unsupported: payload?.unsupported || 0,
    37→		errors: payload?.errors || 0,
    38→	};
    39→}
    40→
    41→function handleModelsUpdatedEvent(payload) {
    42→	if (!payload?.phase) return;
    43→	if (payload.phase === "start") {
    44→		detectingModels.value = true;
    45→		detectError.value = "";
    46→		detectSummary.value = null;
    47→		detectProgress.value = progressFromPayload(payload);
    48→		return;
    49→	}
    50→	if (payload.phase === "progress") {
    51→		detectingModels.value = true;
    52→		detectProgress.value = progressFromPayload(payload);
    53→		return;
    54→	}
    55→	if (payload.phase === "complete") {
    56→		detectingModels.value = false;
    57→		if (payload.summary) {
    58→			detectSummary.value = payload.summary;
    59→			detectProgress.value = progressFromPayload(payload.summary);
    60→		}
    61→		return;
    62→	}
    63→	if (payload.phase === "error") {
    64→		detectingModels.value = false;
    65→		detectError.value = payload.error || t("providers:modelDetectionFailed");
    66→	}
    67→}
    68→
    69→function fetchProviders() {
    70→	loading.value = true;
    71→	return Promise.all([sendRpc("models.list_all", {}), sendRpc("providers.available", {})])
    72→		.then(([modelsRes, providersRes]) => {
    73→			loading.value = false;
    74→			var providerMeta = new Map();
    75→			var configuredProviders = [];
    76→			if (providersRes?.ok) {
    77→				configuredProviders = (providersRes.payload || []).filter(
    78→					(configuredProvider) => configuredProvider.configured,
    79→				);
    80→				for (var providerMetaEntry of providersRes.payload || []) {
    81→					providerMeta.set(providerMetaEntry.name, providerMetaEntry);
    82→				}
    83→			}
    84→			providerMetaSig.value = providerMeta;
    85→
    86→			var models = [];
    87→			if (modelsRes?.ok) {
    88→				models = (modelsRes.payload || []).map((m) => ({
    89→					...m,
    90→					providerDisplayName: providerMeta.get(m.provider)?.displayName || m.provider,
    91→					authType: providerMeta.get(m.provider)?.authType || "api-key",
    92→				}));
    93→			}
    94→
    95→			// Include configured providers that don't currently expose a model.
    96→			var modelProviders = new Set(models.map((m) => m.provider));
    97→			var providerOnlyRows = [];
    98→			providerOnlyRows = configuredProviders
    99→				.filter((providerWithoutModels) => !modelProviders.has(providerWithoutModels.name))
   100→				.map((providerWithoutModels) => ({
   101→					id: `provider:${providerWithoutModels.name}`,
   102→					provider: providerWithoutModels.name,
   103→					displayName: providerWithoutModels.displayName,
   104→					providerDisplayName: providerWithoutModels.displayName,
   105→					providerOnly: true,
   106→					authType: providerWithoutModels.authType,
   107→				}));
   108→
   109→			configuredModels.value = [...models, ...providerOnlyRows];
   110→			updateNavCount("providers", countUniqueProviders(configuredModels.value));
   111→		})
   112→		.catch(() => {
   113→			loading.value = false;
   114→		});
   115→}

   117→async function runDetectAllModels() {
   118→	if (!connected.value || detectingModels.value) return;
   119→	detectingModels.value = true;
   120→	detectSummary.value = null;
   121→	detectError.value = "";
   122→	detectProgress.value = null;
   123→
   124→	try {
   125→		// Phase 1: show current full list first before probing.
   126→		await Promise.all([fetchModels(), fetchProviders()]);
   127→		await new Promise((resolve) => {
   128→			requestAnimationFrame(resolve);
   129→		});
   130→
   131→		var res = await sendRpc("models.detect_supported", {});
   132→		if (!res?.ok) {
   133→			detectError.value = res?.error?.message || t("providers:failedToDetectModels");
   134→			detectingModels.value = false;
   135→			return;
   136→		}
   137→		if (res.payload?.skipped) {
   138→			detectingModels.value = false;
   139→			return;
   140→		}
   141→		detectSummary.value = res.payload || null;
   142→		detectProgress.value = progressFromPayload(res.payload);
   143→		await Promise.all([fetchModels(), fetchProviders()]);
   144→		var p = detectProgress.value;
   145→		if (!p || p.total === 0 || p.checked >= p.total) {
   146→			detectingModels.value = false;
   147→		}
   148→	} catch (_err) {
   149→		detectingModels.value = false;
   150→	}
   151→}
   152→
   153→function groupProviderRows(models, metaMap) {
   154→	var groups = new Map();
   155→	for (var row of models) {
   156→		var key = row.provider;
   157→		if (!groups.has(key)) {
   158→			groups.set(key, {
   159→				provider: key,
   160→				providerDisplayName: row.providerDisplayName || row.displayName || key,
   161→				authType: row.authType || "api-key",
   162→				selectedModel: metaMap?.get(key)?.model || null,
   163→				models: [],
   164→			});
   165→		}
   166→		var groupEntry = groups.get(key);
   167→		if (!row.providerOnly) {
   168→			groupEntry.models.push(row);
   169→		}
   170→	}
   171→
   172→	var result = Array.from(groups.values());
   173→	result.sort((a, b) => {
   174→		var aOrder = metaMap?.get(a.provider)?.uiOrder;
   175→		var bOrder = metaMap?.get(b.provider)?.uiOrder;
   176→		var hasAOrder = Number.isFinite(aOrder);
   177→		var hasBOrder = Number.isFinite(bOrder);
   178→		if (hasAOrder && hasBOrder && aOrder !== bOrder) return aOrder - bOrder;
   179→		if (hasAOrder && !hasBOrder) return -1;
   180→		if (!hasAOrder && hasBOrder) return 1;
   181→		return a.providerDisplayName.localeCompare(b.providerDisplayName);
   182→	});
   183→	for (var providerGroup of result) {
   184→		providerGroup.models.sort((a, b) => {
   185→			var aTime = a.createdAt || 0;
   186→			var bTime = b.createdAt || 0;
   187→			if (aTime !== bTime) return bTime - aTime;
   188→			return (a.displayName || a.id).localeCompare(b.displayName || b.id);
   189→		});
   190→	}
   191→	return result;
   192→}

   357→					if (loading.value && configuredModels.value.length === 0) {
   358→						return html`<div id="providersLoadingState" class="text-xs text-[var(--muted)]">${t("common:status.loading")}</div>`;
   359→					}
   360→					if (configuredModels.value.length === 0) {
   361→						return html`<div id="providersEmptyState" data-testid="providers-empty-state" class="text-xs text-[var(--muted)]" style="padding:12px 0;">${t("providers:noProvidersConfigured")}</div>`;
   362→					}
   363→					return html`<div id="providersConfiguredList" data-testid="providers-configured-list" style="max-width:600px;">
   364→						${
   365→							groups.length > 1
   366→								? html`<div class="flex flex-wrap gap-1 mb-3">
   367→							${groups.map(
   368→								(g) => html`<button
   369→								key=${g.provider}
   370→								class="text-xs px-2 py-1 rounded-md border border-[var(--border)] bg-[var(--surface)] text-[var(--muted)] hover:text-[var(--text)] hover:border-[var(--border-strong)] cursor-pointer"
   371→								onClick=${() => {
   372→									var el = document.getElementById(`provider-${g.provider}`);
   373→									if (el) el.scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth", block: "start" });
   374→								}}
   375→							>${g.providerDisplayName}<span class="ml-1 opacity-60">${g.models.length}</span></button>`,
   376→							)}
   377→						</div>`
   378→								: null
   379→						}
   380→						<div style="display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:6px;margin-bottom:12px;">
   381→							${groups.map((g) => html`<${ProviderSection} key=${g.provider} group=${g} />`)}
   382→						</div>
   383→					</div>`;
   384→				})()}
   385→			</div>
   386→		<${ConfirmDialog} />
   387→		`;
   388→}
   389→
   390→var _providersContainer = null;
   391→
   392→export function initProviders(container) {
   393→	_providersContainer = container;
   394→	container.style.cssText = "flex-direction:column;padding:0;overflow:hidden;";
   395→	render(html`<${ProvidersPage} />`, container);
   396→}
   397→
   398→export function teardownProviders() {
   399→	S.setRefreshProvidersPage(null);
   400→	if (_providersContainer) render(null, _providersContainer);
   401→	_providersContainer = null;
   402→}
   403→

   908→					</div>`

     1→-- Sessions table schema
     2→-- Owned by: moltis-sessions crate
     3→-- Depends on: moltis-projects (sessions.project_id references projects.id)
     4→
     5→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (
     6→    key             TEXT    PRIMARY KEY,
     7→    id              TEXT    NOT NULL,
     8→    label           TEXT,
     9→    model           TEXT,
    10→    created_at      INTEGER NOT NULL,
    11→    updated_at      INTEGER NOT NULL,
    12→    message_count   INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    13→    project_id      TEXT    REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    14→    archived        INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    15→    worktree_branch TEXT,
    16→    sandbox_enabled INTEGER,
    17→    sandbox_image   TEXT,
    18→    channel_binding TEXT
    19→);
    20→
    21→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_created_at ON sessions(created_at);
    22→
    23→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS channel_sessions (
    24→    channel_type TEXT    NOT NULL,
    25→    account_id   TEXT    NOT NULL,
    26→    chat_id      TEXT    NOT NULL,
    27→    session_key  TEXT    NOT NULL,
    28→    updated_at   INTEGER NOT NULL,
    29→    PRIMARY KEY (channel_type, account_id, chat_id)
    30→);
    31→
```

> AGENT

Good. I have a clear picture now. Here's my plan:

**Immediate fix (settings page):** Show only the top 3 most recent models per provider, with a "Show all N models" toggle. Models are already sorted by `createdAt` desc, so the most recent are first.

**For popularity:** The `sessions` table already stores a `model` column per session. We can add an RPC endpoint that queries `SELECT model, COUNT(*) FROM sessions WHERE model IS NOT NULL GROUP BY model` and use those counts to sort/badge popular models. This gives real usage-based popularity.

Let me implement the collapsible model list first.

Exit code 1
Error: failed to open database: database "moltis" not found on Dolt server at 127.0.0.1:54747

Common causes:
  - Switched git branches (the Dolt database is runtime state, not in git)
  - The server is serving a different data directory than expected
  - The server was restarted and is using a different port
  - Another project's Dolt server is running on this port

To diagnose:
  bd doctor                  # Check server and database health
  bd dolt status             # Show which data directory the server is using

Tip: To bootstrap from an existing Dolt remote, set sync.git-remote
in .beads/config.yaml and re-run bd init.

   112→	test("custom local model download progress modal renders without JS errors", async ({ page }) => {
   113→		const pageErrors = watchPageErrors(page);

   116→		await page.evaluate(async () => {
   117→			const [providers, events, state] = await Promise.all([

   120→				import("/assets/js/state.js"),
   121→			]);
   122→
   123→			state.setWs({
   124→				readyState: WebSocket.OPEN,
   125→				send(raw) {
   126→					const frame = JSON.parse(raw);
   127→					const respond = (payload) => {
   128→						queueMicrotask(() => {
   129→							const pending = state.pending[frame.id];
   130→							if (!pending) return;
   131→							pending(payload);
   132→							delete state.pending[frame.id];
   133→						});
   134→					};
   135→
   136→					if (frame.method === "providers.local.status") {
   137→						respond({ ok: true, payload: { status: "ready", model_id: "custom-test" } });
   138→						return;
   139→					}
   140→
   141→					if (frame.method === "models.list" || frame.method === "providers.available") {
   142→						respond({ ok: true, payload: [] });
   143→						return;
   144→					}
   145→
   146→					respond({ ok: true, payload: {} });
   147→				},
   148→			});
   149→
   150→			providers.showModelDownloadProgress({ id: "custom-test", displayName: "Custom.gguf" }, { name: "local-llm" });

   152→			const listeners = events.eventListeners["local-llm.download"] || [];
   153→			for (const listener of listeners.slice()) {
   154→				listener({

   156→					progress: 50,

   165→		await expect

   167→			.toContain("Custom.gguf configured successfully!");

> AGENT

All clean. Let me summarize the changes and answer the popularity question.

---

**Changes made** (settings page, `page-providers.js`):

- Each provider section now shows only the **3 most recent** models by default (models are already sorted newest-first by `createdAt`)
- A "Show N more models..." link appears below the visible models to expand the full list
- Clicking again collapses back to 3
- i18n strings added in en/fr/zh

This turns OpenAI's 111-model list into 3 visible + "Show 108 more models..." -- massively less overwhelming.

**The onboarding page** already gates model selection behind a "Choose Model" button per provider, so the individual model flood isn't visible on the main onboarding screen.

---

**For model popularity**, the best data source is already in the codebase: the `sessions` table has a `model TEXT` column tracking which model each session uses. A future `models.usage_stats` RPC could query:

```sql
SELECT model, COUNT(*) as usage_count
FROM sessions
WHERE model IS NOT NULL
GROUP BY model
ORDER BY usage_count DESC
```

This gives **real usage-based popularity** for the specific user's instance. You could then:
1. Sort models within each provider by usage count (most-used first) instead of just `createdAt`
2. Show a subtle "Most used" badge on the top 1-2 models per provider
3. In the collapsed view, prioritize showing the user's most-used models rather than just the newest

Want me to implement the popularity feature as well?

> DEVELOPER

Issue is, you want to know popularity before the user selects it, once selected I don't think they'll often go to settings.

> AGENT

Good point. Pre-selection popularity needs to come from outside the user's own usage data. Options:

1. **Hardcoded tiers in the provider code** — each provider already defines its model list with `display_name`, `created_at`, etc. We could add a `tier` or `recommended` field (e.g. "flagship", "fast", "legacy") to `ModelInfo`. Providers know which models are their flagships. This is the simplest and most reliable — no external calls needed.

2. **Fetch from provider APIs** — some providers expose popularity/usage metadata. OpenAI's `/v1/models` doesn't, Anthropic's doesn't. Dead end for most.

3. **Crowdsourced data** — call an external API like OpenRouter's model rankings or a community leaderboard. Adds a dependency and network call, fragile.

4. **Static curated list shipped with the binary** — a JSON/TOML mapping of `model_id -> tier` that gets updated with each release. Easy to maintain, no runtime cost.

Option 1 is the cleanest fit for this codebase. Let me check what `ModelInfo` looks like and how providers build their model lists.

   340→pub async fn fetch_models_from_api(
   341→    api_key: secrecy::Secret<String>,
   342→    base_url: String,
   343→) -> anyhow::Result<Vec<super::DiscoveredModel>> {
   344→    let client = crate::shared_http_client();
   345→    let response = client
   346→        .get(models_endpoint(&base_url))
   347→        .timeout(Duration::from_secs(8))
   348→        .header(
   349→            "Authorization",
   350→            format!("Bearer {}", api_key.expose_secret()),
   351→        )
   352→        .header("Accept", "application/json")
   353→        .send()
   354→        .await?;
   355→    let status = response.status();
   356→    let body = response.text().await?;
   357→    if !status.is_success() {
   358→        anyhow::bail!("openai models API error HTTP {status}");
   359→    }
   360→    let payload: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&body)?;
   361→    let models = parse_models_payload(&payload);
   362→    if models.is_empty() {
   363→        anyhow::bail!("openai models API returned no models");
   364→    }
   365→    Ok(models)
   366→}
   367→
   368→/// Spawn model discovery in a background thread and return the receiver
   369→/// immediately, without blocking. Call `.recv()` later to collect the result.
   370→pub fn start_model_discovery(
   371→    api_key: secrecy::Secret<String>,
   372→    base_url: String,
   373→) -> mpsc::Receiver<anyhow::Result<Vec<super::DiscoveredModel>>> {
   374→    let (tx, rx) = mpsc::sync_channel(1);
   375→    std::thread::spawn(move || {
   376→        let result = tokio::runtime::Builder::new_current_thread()
   377→            .enable_all()
   378→            .build()
   379→            .map_err(anyhow::Error::from)
   380→            .and_then(|rt| rt.block_on(fetch_models_from_api(api_key, base_url)));
   381→        let _ = tx.send(result);
   382→    });
   383→    rx
   384→}
   385→
   386→fn fetch_models_blocking(
   387→    api_key: secrecy::Secret<String>,
   388→    base_url: String,
   389→) -> anyhow::Result<Vec<super::DiscoveredModel>> {
   390→    start_model_discovery(api_key, base_url)
   391→        .recv()
   392→        .map_err(|err| anyhow::anyhow!("openai model discovery worker failed: {err}"))?
   393→}
   394→
   395→pub fn live_models(
   396→    api_key: &secrecy::Secret<String>,
   397→    base_url: &str,
   398→) -> anyhow::Result<Vec<super::DiscoveredModel>> {
   399→    let models = fetch_models_blocking(api_key.clone(), base_url.to_string())?;
   400→    debug!(model_count = models.len(), "loaded live models");
   401→    Ok(models)
   402→}
   403→
   404→#[must_use]
   405→pub fn available_models(
   406→    api_key: &secrecy::Secret<String>,
   407→    base_url: &str,
   408→) -> Vec<super::DiscoveredModel> {
   409→    let fallback = default_model_catalog();
   410→    if cfg!(test) {
   411→        return fallback;
   412→    }
   413→
   414→    let discovered = match live_models(api_key, base_url) {
   415→        Ok(models) => models,
   416→        Err(err) => {
   417→            warn!(error = %err, base_url = %base_url, "failed to fetch openai models, using fallback catalog");
   418→            return fallback;
   419→        },

  1726→#[async_trait]
  1727→impl ModelService for LiveModelService {
  1728→    async fn list(&self) -> ServiceResult {
  1729→        let reg = self.providers.read().await;
  1730→        let disabled = self.disabled.read().await;
  1731→        let order = self.priority_order().await;
  1732→        let all_models = reg.list_models_with_reasoning_variants();
  1733→        let prioritized = Self::prioritize_models(
  1734→            &order,
  1735→            all_models
  1736→                .iter()
  1737→                .filter(|m| moltis_providers::is_chat_capable_model(&m.id))
  1738→                .filter(|m| !disabled.is_disabled(&m.id))
  1739→                .filter(|m| disabled.unsupported_info(&m.id).is_none()),
  1740→        );
  1741→        info!(model_count = prioritized.len(), "models.list response");
  1742→        let models: Vec<_> = prioritized
  1743→            .iter()
  1744→            .copied()
  1745→            .map(|m| {
  1746→                let supports_tools = reg.get(&m.id).is_some_and(|p| p.supports_tools());
  1747→                let preferred = Self::priority_rank(&order, m) != usize::MAX;
  1748→                serde_json::json!({
  1749→                    "id": m.id,
  1750→                    "provider": m.provider,
  1751→                    "displayName": m.display_name,
  1752→                    "supportsTools": supports_tools,
  1753→                    "preferred": preferred,
  1754→                    "createdAt": m.created_at,
  1755→                    "unsupported": false,
  1756→                    "unsupportedReason": Value::Null,
  1757→                    "unsupportedProvider": Value::Null,
  1758→                    "unsupportedUpdatedAt": Value::Null,
  1759→                })
  1760→            })
  1761→            .collect();
  1762→        Ok(serde_json::json!(models))
  1763→    }
  1764→
  1765→    async fn list_all(&self) -> ServiceResult {
  1766→        let reg = self.providers.read().await;
  1767→        let disabled = self.disabled.read().await;
  1768→        let order = self.priority_order().await;
  1769→        let all_models = reg.list_models_with_reasoning_variants();
  1770→        let prioritized = Self::prioritize_models(
  1771→            &order,
  1772→            all_models
  1773→                .iter()
  1774→                .filter(|m| moltis_providers::is_chat_capable_model(&m.id)),
  1775→        );
  1776→        info!(model_count = prioritized.len(), "models.list_all response");
  1777→        let models: Vec<_> = prioritized
  1778→            .iter()
  1779→            .copied()
  1780→            .map(|m| {
  1781→                let supports_tools = reg.get(&m.id).is_some_and(|p| p.supports_tools());
  1782→                let unsupported = disabled.unsupported_info(&m.id);
  1783→                serde_json::json!({
  1784→                    "id": m.id,
  1785→                    "provider": m.provider,
  1786→                    "displayName": m.display_name,
  1787→                    "supportsTools": supports_tools,
  1788→                    "createdAt": m.created_at,
  1789→                    "disabled": disabled.is_disabled(&m.id),
  1790→                    "unsupported": unsupported.is_some(),
  1791→                    "unsupportedReason": unsupported.map(|u| u.detail.clone()),
  1792→                    "unsupportedProvider": unsupported.and_then(|u| u.provider.clone()),
  1793→                    "unsupportedUpdatedAt": unsupported.map(|u| u.updated_at_ms),
  1794→                })
  1795→            })
  1796→            .collect();
  1797→        Ok(serde_json::json!(models))
  1798→    }

> AGENT

The cleanest approach: add a `recommended` flag to `ModelInfo`/`DiscoveredModel`. For static catalogs (Anthropic, MiniMax, etc.), the first 2-3 entries are already the flagships. For dynamic models (OpenAI), we can pattern-match known flagships (latest generation, no date suffix). No external API needed.

But first, let me improve the collapse to be smarter — right now it just takes the first 3 by `createdAt`. It should show **preferred** (user-selected) models first, then fall back to most recent. That way the collapsed view shows what the user actually uses.

  1728→    async fn list(&self) -> ServiceResult {
  1729→        let reg = self.providers.read().await;
  1730→        let disabled = self.disabled.read().await;
  1731→        let order = self.priority_order().await;
  1732→        let all_models = reg.list_models_with_reasoning_variants();
  1733→        let prioritized = Self::prioritize_models(
  1734→            &order,
  1735→            all_models
  1736→                .iter()
  1737→                .filter(|m| moltis_providers::is_chat_capable_model(&m.id))
  1738→                .filter(|m| !disabled.is_disabled(&m.id))
  1739→                .filter(|m| disabled.unsupported_info(&m.id).is_none()),
  1740→        );
  1741→        info!(model_count = prioritized.len(), "models.list response");
  1742→        let models: Vec<_> = prioritized
  1743→            .iter()
  1744→            .copied()
  1745→            .map(|m| {
  1746→                let supports_tools = reg.get(&m.id).is_some_and(|p| p.supports_tools());
  1747→                let preferred = Self::priority_rank(&order, m) != usize::MAX;
  1748→                serde_json::json!({
  1749→                    "id": m.id,
  1750→                    "provider": m.provider,
  1751→                    "displayName": m.display_name,

  1759→                })
  1760→            })

  1492→pub struct LiveModelService {
  1493→    providers: Arc<RwLock<ProviderRegistry>>,
  1494→    disabled: Arc<RwLock<DisabledModelsStore>>,
  1495→    state: Arc<OnceCell<Arc<dyn ChatRuntime>>>,
  1496→    detect_gate: Arc<Semaphore>,
  1497→    priority_models: Arc<RwLock<Vec<String>>>,
  1498→}
  1499→
  1500→impl LiveModelService {
  1501→    pub fn new(
  1502→        providers: Arc<RwLock<ProviderRegistry>>,
  1503→        disabled: Arc<RwLock<DisabledModelsStore>>,
  1504→        priority_models: Vec<String>,
  1505→    ) -> Self {
  1506→        Self {
  1507→            providers,
  1508→            disabled,
  1509→            state: Arc::new(OnceCell::new()),
  1510→            detect_gate: Arc::new(Semaphore::new(1)),
  1511→            priority_models: Arc::new(RwLock::new(priority_models)),
  1512→        }
  1513→    }
  1514→
  1515→    /// Shared handle to the priority models list. Pass this to services
  1516→    /// that need to update model ordering at runtime (e.g. `save_model`).
  1517→    pub fn priority_models_handle(&self) -> Arc<RwLock<Vec<String>>> {
  1518→        Arc::clone(&self.priority_models)
  1519→    }
  1520→
  1521→    fn build_priority_order(models: &[String]) -> HashMap<String, usize> {
  1522→        let mut order = HashMap::new();
  1523→        for (idx, model) in models.iter().enumerate() {
  1524→            let key = normalize_model_key(model);
  1525→            if !key.is_empty() {
  1526→                let _ = order.entry(key).or_insert(idx);
  1527→            }
  1528→        }
  1529→        order
  1530→    }
  1531→
  1532→    fn priority_rank(order: &HashMap<String, usize>, model: &moltis_providers::ModelInfo) -> usize {
  1533→        let full = normalize_model_key(&model.id);
  1534→        if let Some(rank) = order.get(&full) {
  1535→            return *rank;
  1536→        }
  1537→        let raw = normalize_model_key(raw_model_id(&model.id));
  1538→        if let Some(rank) = order.get(&raw) {
  1539→            return *rank;
  1540→        }
  1541→        let display = normalize_model_key(&model.display_name);
  1542→        if let Some(rank) = order.get(&display) {
  1543→            return *rank;
  1544→        }
  1545→        usize::MAX
  1546→    }
  1547→
  1548→    fn prioritize_models<'a>(
  1549→        order: &HashMap<String, usize>,
  1550→        models: impl Iterator<Item = &'a moltis_providers::ModelInfo>,
  1551→    ) -> Vec<&'a moltis_providers::ModelInfo> {

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/succinct-pyroraptor/crates/chat/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "    async fn list(&self) -> ServiceResult {\n        let reg = self.providers.read().await;\n        let disabled = self.disabled.read().await;\n        let order = self.priority_order().await;\n        let all_models = reg.list_models_with_reasoning_variants();\n        let prioritized = Self::prioritize_models(\n            &order,\n            all_models\n                .iter()\n                .filter(|m| moltis_providers::is_chat_capable_model(&m.id))\n                .filter(|m| !disabled.is_disabled(&m.id))\n                .filter(|m| disabled.unsupported_info(&m.id).is_none()),\n        );\n        info!(model_count = prioritized.len(), \"models.list response\");\n        let models: Vec<_> = prioritized\n            .iter()\n            .copied()\n            .map(|m| {\n                let supports_tools = reg.get(&m.id).is_some_and(|p| p.supports_tools());\n                let preferred = Self::priority_rank(&order, m) != usize::MAX;\n                serde_json::json!({\n                    \"id\": m.id,\n                    \"provider\": m.provider,\n                    \"displayName\": m.display_name,\n                    \"supportsTools\": supports_tools,\n                    \"preferred\": preferred,\n                    \"createdAt\": m.created_at,\n                    \"unsupported\": false,\n                    \"unsupportedReason\": Value::Null,\n                    \"unsupportedProvider\": Value::Null,\n                    \"unsupportedUpdatedAt\": Value::Null,\n                })\n            })\n            .collect();\n        Ok(serde_json::json!(models))",
  "new_string": "    async fn list(&self) -> ServiceResult {\n        let reg = self.providers.read().await;\n        let disabled = self.disabled.read().await;\n        let order = self.priority_order().await;\n        let all_models = reg.list_models_with_reasoning_variants();\n\n        // Hide models older than 1 year from the chat selector unless the\n        // user opted in via `providers.show_legacy_models`.  Preferred models\n        // and models without a timestamp are never hidden.\n        let legacy_cutoff: Option<i64> = if self.show_legacy_models {\n            None\n        } else {\n            let one_year_ago = std::time::SystemTime::now()\n                .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)\n                .unwrap_or_default()\n                .as_secs() as i64\n                - 365 * 24 * 60 * 60;\n            Some(one_year_ago)\n        };\n\n        let prioritized = Self::prioritize_models(\n            &order,\n            all_models\n                .iter()\n                .filter(|m| moltis_providers::is_chat_capable_model(&m.id))\n                .filter(|m| !disabled.is_disabled(&m.id))\n                .filter(|m| disabled.unsupported_info(&m.id).is_none()),\n        );\n        info!(model_count = prioritized.len(), \"models.list response\");\n        let models: Vec<_> = prioritized\n            .iter()\n            .copied()\n            .filter(|m| {\n                let preferred = Self::priority_rank(&order, m) != usize::MAX;\n                if preferred {\n                    return true;\n                }\n                match (legacy_cutoff, m.created_at) {\n                    (Some(cutoff), Some(ts)) => ts >= cutoff,\n                    _ => true, // no cutoff or no timestamp → keep\n                }\n            })\n            .map(|m| {\n                let supports_tools = reg.get(&m.id).is_some_and(|p| p.supports_tools());\n                let preferred = Self::priority_rank(&order, m) != usize::MAX;\n                serde_json::json!({\n                    \"id\": m.id,\n                    \"provider\": m.provider,\n                    \"displayName\": m.display_name,\n                    \"supportsTools\": supports_tools,\n                    \"preferred\": preferred,\n                    \"createdAt\": m.created_at,\n                    \"unsupported\": false,\n                    \"unsupportedReason\": Value::Null,\n                    \"unsupportedProvider\": Value::Null,\n                    \"unsupportedUpdatedAt\": Value::Null,\n                })\n            })\n            .collect();\n        Ok(serde_json::json!(models))"
}

> AGENT

Here's a summary of all changes:

### Settings page (`page-providers.js`)
- **Collapse model lists**: Shows only the top 3 models per provider by default, with a "Show N more models..." toggle
- **Preferred models first**: Sort puts preferred (user-selected) models at the top of each provider, so they're always visible in the collapsed view
- **Preferred badge**: Preferred models get a visible "Preferred" badge (uses existing `recommended-badge` CSS class)

### Chat model selector (`models.list` RPC)
- **Hide legacy models**: Models older than 1 year are automatically hidden from the chat model selector
- **Preferred exempt**: Preferred models are never hidden regardless of age
- **No-timestamp exempt**: Models without `created_at` (static catalog entries) are never hidden
- **Config override**: `providers.show_legacy_models = true` in `moltis.toml` to show all models

### Backend (`list_all` RPC)
- **Added `preferred` field**: `models.list_all` now includes `preferred: bool` so the settings page can sort by it (previously only `models.list` had it)

### Config (`schema.rs`, `validate.rs`, `template.rs`)
- **New field**: `providers.show_legacy_models` (default `false`)
- **Schema map updated** per CLAUDE.md requirements

### i18n (en/fr/zh)
- Added: `preferred`, `showAllModels`, `showFewerModels`

### Tests
- Existing `list_all` test extended to verify `preferred` field
- New: `list_hides_legacy_models_by_default` — verifies 2-year-old model hidden, recent model shown, `list_all` still shows both
- New: `list_shows_legacy_models_when_configured` — verifies config override works

---

**On popularity before selection**: The best approach for this project is adding a `recommended` field to `ModelInfo` in the Rust provider code. Static catalogs already have curated order (flagships first). For API-discovered models (OpenAI), pattern-matching on known flagship IDs (e.g., latest generation without date suffixes) would work. This is the next logical step — no external API dependency, no runtime cost, ships with each release.

  "new_string": "#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct DiscoveredModel {\n    pub id: String,\n    pub display_name: String,\n    /// Unix timestamp from the API (e.g. OpenAI `created` field).\n    /// Used to sort models newest-first. `None` for static catalog entries.\n    pub created_at: Option<i64>,\n    /// Flagged by the provider as a recommended/flagship model.\n    /// Used to surface the most relevant models in the UI.\n    pub recommended: bool,\n}\n\nimpl DiscoveredModel {\n    pub fn new(id: impl Into<String>, display_name: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            id: id.into(),\n            display_name: display_name.into(),\n            created_at: None,\n            recommended: false,\n        }\n    }\n\n    pub fn with_created_at(mut self, created_at: Option<i64>) -> Self {\n        self.created_at = created_at;\n        self\n    }\n\n    pub fn with_recommended(mut self, recommended: bool) -> Self {\n        self.recommended = recommended;\n        self\n    }\n}\n\n/// Convert a static model catalog into `DiscoveredModel` entries, marking\n/// the first `recommended_count` as recommended.\nfn catalog_to_discovered(\n    catalog: &[(&str, &str)],\n    recommended_count: usize,\n) -> Vec<DiscoveredModel> {\n    catalog\n        .iter()\n        .enumerate()\n        .map(|(i, (id, name))| {\n            DiscoveredModel::new(*id, *name).with_recommended(i < recommended_count)\n        })\n        .collect()\n}"
}

   163→fn parse_model_entry(entry: &serde_json::Value) -> Option<super::DiscoveredModel> {
   164→    let obj = entry.as_object()?;
   165→    let model_id = obj
   166→        .get("id")
   167→        .or_else(|| obj.get("slug"))
   168→        .or_else(|| obj.get("model"))
   169→        .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)?;
   170→
   171→    if !is_likely_model_id(model_id) {
   172→        return None;
   173→    }
   174→
   175→    let display_name = obj
   176→        .get("display_name")
   177→        .or_else(|| obj.get("displayName"))
   178→        .or_else(|| obj.get("name"))
   179→        .or_else(|| obj.get("title"))
   180→        .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str);
   181→
   182→    let created_at = obj.get("created").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_i64);
   183→
   184→    Some(
   185→        super::DiscoveredModel::new(model_id, normalize_display_name(model_id, display_name))
   186→            .with_created_at(created_at),
   187→    )
   188→}
   189→
   190→fn collect_candidate_arrays<'a>(
   191→    value: &'a serde_json::Value,
   192→    out: &mut Vec<&'a serde_json::Value>,
   193→) {
   194→    match value {
   195→        serde_json::Value::Array(items) => out.extend(items),
   196→        serde_json::Value::Object(map) => {
   197→            for key in ["models", "data", "items", "results", "available"] {
   198→                if let Some(nested) = map.get(key) {
   199→                    collect_candidate_arrays(nested, out);
   200→                }
   201→            }
   202→        },

  "new_string": "    let recommended = is_recommended_openai_model(model_id);\n    Some(\n        super::DiscoveredModel::new(model_id, normalize_display_name(model_id, display_name))\n            .with_created_at(created_at)\n            .with_recommended(recommended),\n    )\n}\n\n/// Known OpenAI flagship model IDs (latest generation, no date suffix).\n/// These are the models most users care about.\nfn is_recommended_openai_model(model_id: &str) -> bool {\n    matches!(\n        model_id,\n        \"gpt-5.4\" | \"gpt-5.4-mini\" | \"gpt-5.4-pro\" | \"o4-mini\" | \"o3\"\n    )\n}"
}

Let me read each test block with the error and fix them. There are 8 locations — let me batch them with a targeted sed since they're all `created_at: None,\n            },` or similar:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01BiSGUxBeP5g8oxmio3AR5V
```json
{
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport re\nwith open('crates/providers/src/lib.rs', 'r') as f:\n    content = f.read()\n\n# Find all ModelInfo { ... } blocks missing 'recommended' in test code (after line ~2970)\n# Pattern: 'created_at: ...,\\n...}' without 'recommended' on the next line\nlines = content.split('\\n')\nfixed = []\ni = 0\nwhile i < len(lines):\n    fixed.append(lines[i])\n    # Check if this line has 'created_at:' and is inside a ModelInfo block\n    stripped = lines[i].strip()\n    if stripped.startswith('created_at:') and stripped.endswith(','):\n        # Check if the next non-empty line closes the struct (starts with })\n        # and doesn't already have 'recommended'\n        j = i + 1\n        while j < len(lines) and lines[j].strip() == '':\n            j += 1\n        if j < len(lines) and lines[j].strip().startswith('}') and 'recommended' not in lines[i-1] and 'recommended' not in lines[i]:\n            # Check this is not already fixed\n            has_recommended = False\n            for k in range(max(0,i-5), i+1):\n                if 'recommended' in lines[k]:\n                    has_recommended = True\n                    break\n            if not has_recommended:\n                # Add recommended: false with same indentation\n                indent = len(lines[i]) - len(lines[i].lstrip())\n                fixed.append(' ' * indent + 'recommended: false,')\n    i += 1\n\nwith open('crates/providers/src/lib.rs', 'w') as f:\n    f.write('\\n'.join(fixed))\nprint('Done')\n\"\n",
  "description": "Add recommended: false to all remaining ModelInfo test blocks"
}
```

> AGENT

Here's the complete summary of everything implemented:

### Settings page model list
- **Collapsed by default**: Only 3 models shown per provider, "Show N more models..." toggle
- **Sort order**: Preferred > Recommended > Most recent > Alphabetical
- **Preferred badge**: Shows "Preferred" on user-selected models
- i18n in en/fr/zh

### Chat model selector (backend)
- **Legacy model hiding**: Models older than 1 year hidden from chat dropdown
- **Exempt**: Preferred models, models without timestamps never hidden
- **Config**: `providers.show_legacy_models = true` in `moltis.toml` to override
- `list_all` (settings page) still shows everything

### Recommended models (new field)
- `recommended: bool` on `DiscoveredModel` and `ModelInfo`
- Serialized to frontend as `"recommended": true/false` in both `models.list` and `models.list_all`
- **Static catalogs**: First N entries marked recommended via `catalog_to_discovered()`:
  - Anthropic: top 3 (Opus 4.5, Sonnet 4.5, Haiku 4.5)
  - OpenAI Codex: top 3
  - GitHub Copilot: top 3
  - OpenAI-compatible (MiniMax, Z.AI, etc.): top 2
  - Kimi Code: top 1
- **OpenAI API-discovered**: Pattern-matching flagships: `gpt-5.4`, `gpt-5.4-mini`, `gpt-5.4-pro`, `o4-mini`, `o3`
- Reasoning variants (`@reasoning-low/medium/high`) are not recommended (too noisy)
- Settings page sorts recommended models higher in collapsed view

    23→
    24→	if (
    25→		lower.includes("401") ||
    26→		lower.includes("unauthorized") ||
    27→		lower.includes("invalid api key") ||

    30→		return "Invalid API key. Please double-check and try again.";
    31→	}

    47→
    48→	return error;

> AGENT

Found it. Line 32: `lower.includes("permission")` matches "You do not have **permission** to access glm-5.1" and replaces it with the generic "Your API key doesn't have access. Check your account permissions."

But in the HTML the user showed, the tooltip says "Service temporarily unavailable" — that's a different error, probably from an earlier probe. The `humanizeProbeError` doesn't have a pattern for "temporarily unavailable", so it falls through to returning the raw error (line 48).

Two fixes needed:
1. Show the error inline (not tooltip-only) in `ModelSelectCard`
2. The `humanizeProbeError` is overly generic — "permission" matching is too broad. But that's a separate issue. The user's main ask is showing the error inline.

  1127→	m.body.textContent = "";

  1131→	// Track per-model probe state: "probing" | "ok" | { error: string }
  1132→	var probeResults = new Map();
  1133→
  1134→	function probeModel(modelId) {
  1135→		if (probeResults.has(modelId)) return;

  1138→		testModel(modelId).then((result) => {
  1139→			if (isModelServiceNotConfigured(result.error || "")) {
  1140→				// Model service not ready — don't flag as broken.
  1141→				probeResults.delete(modelId);
  1142→			} else if (!result.ok && isTimeoutError(result.error || "")) {

> AGENT

No direct use. So the "Service temporarily unavailable" IS coming from the live probe. The `humanizeProbeError` doesn't match "temporarily unavailable" so it returns the raw error. But the raw error from Z.AI API might actually be "Service temporarily unavailable. Please try again." — not "You do not have permission to access glm-5.1".

The backend `parse_chat_error` extracts a `detail` field from the error, which becomes "You do not have permission to access glm-5.1". But the original HTTP error body might contain "Service temporarily unavailable" and `parse_chat_error` transforms it differently than what the frontend sees.

Let me check — the probe could be hitting the provider twice (retry), getting different errors each time. Or the `parse_chat_error` extracts a detail that differs from the raw message:

    11→pub fn parse_chat_error(raw: &str, provider_name: Option<&str>) -> Value {
    12→    let mut error = try_parse_known_error(raw);
    13→
    14→    if let Some(name) = provider_name
    15→        && let Some(obj) = error.as_object_mut()
    16→    {
    17→        obj.insert("provider".into(), Value::String(name.to_string()));
    18→    }
    19→
    20→    error
    21→}
    22→
    23→fn try_parse_known_error(raw: &str) -> Value {
    24→    let http_status = extract_http_status(raw);
    25→
    26→    // Try to extract embedded JSON from the error string.
    27→    if let Some(start) = raw.find('{')
    28→        && let Ok(parsed) = serde_json::from_str::<Value>(&raw[start..])
    29→    {
    30→        let err_obj = parsed.get("error").unwrap_or(&parsed);
    31→
    32→        // Usage limit
    33→        if matches_type_or_message(err_obj, "usage_limit_reached", "usage limit") {
    34→            let plan_type = err_obj
    35→                .get("plan_type")
    36→                .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
    37→                .unwrap_or("current");
    38→            let resets_at = extract_resets_at(err_obj);
    39→            return build_error(
    40→                "usage_limit_reached",
    41→                "",
    42→                "Usage limit reached",
    43→                &format!("Your {} plan limit has been reached.", plan_type),
    44→                resets_at,
    45→                None,
    46→                Some(serde_json::json!({ "planType": plan_type })),
    47→            );
    48→        }
    49→
    50→        // Billing / quota exhaustion (not transient rate limiting).
    51→        if is_insufficient_quota_error(err_obj, raw) {
    52→            let detail = err_obj.get("message").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or(
    53→                "Your account quota is exhausted. Add funds or switch providers and try again.",
    54→            );
    55→            return build_error(
    56→                "billing_exhausted",
    57→                "\u{26A0}\u{FE0F}",
    58→                "Insufficient quota",
    59→                detail,
    60→                None,

Found it. Line 51: **All non-Forbidden `ServiceError`s get error code `UNAVAILABLE`**. The probe returns `Err(detail.into())` which creates a `ServiceError::Message`, which converts to `ErrorShape` with code `UNAVAILABLE`. Then `localizedRpcErrorMessage` at line 196-201 maps `error.code` → `errors:codes.UNAVAILABLE` → "Service temporarily unavailable. Please try again."

The localized message **replaces** the actual error text. The fix: the frontend `testModel` should use the raw `error.message`, not the localized version, since `sendRpc` resolves with the raw frame (which includes `error.code` AND `error.message`).

> AGENT

**Line 96**: `frame.error = localizeRpcError(frame.error)` — the WS handler **localizes every error response** before passing it to the pending callback. `localizeRpcError` sees `code: "UNAVAILABLE"`, looks up `errors:codes.UNAVAILABLE` → "Service temporarily unavailable. Please try again.", and **replaces** `frame.error.message` with it.

So the actual error "You do not have permission to access glm-5.1" gets overwritten with the generic locale string before `testModel` ever sees it.

The fix: `localizeRpcError` should only replace the message when there isn't already a meaningful one from the server. Or `testModel` should look at the raw message before localization.

The cleanest fix is in `localizeRpcError` — don't replace when the server already provided a specific message:

The problem is `localizedRpcErrorMessage` at line 196-201: if `error.code` has a translation, it returns the translation and **ignores** `error.message`. The fix should preserve the original message when the server provides a specific one, and only fall back to the locale string for truly generic codes:

> TOOL

```
34395954 fix(web): preserve server error message for model probes
```

> AGENT

Pushed. The root cause was:

1. Backend returns `Err(detail)` → becomes `ServiceError::Message` → serialized with `code: "UNAVAILABLE"` 
2. `ws-connect.js` line 96 calls `localizeRpcError` on **every** WS response, which maps `UNAVAILABLE` → "Service temporarily unavailable. Please try again."
3. The actual error "You do not have permission to access glm-5.1" was lost

The fix preserves the original server message as `serverMessage` on the error object, and `testModel` now prefers `serverMessage` over the localized `message`. This way you'll see the actual backend error.

> AGENT

Push confirmed. The model probe should now show "You do not have permission to access glm-5.1" instead of "Service temporarily unavailable."

{

  "old_string": "// ── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────\nimport { hasTranslation, t } from \"./i18n.js\";\nimport * as S from \"./state.js\";",
  "new_string": "// ── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────\nimport { hasTranslation, t } from \"./i18n.js\";\nimport * as S from \"./state.js\";\n\n/**\n * Extract the highest version number from a model ID for sorting.\n * e.g. \"gpt-5.4-mini\" → 5.4, \"claude-opus-4-6-20260301\" → 4.6, \"o4-mini\" → 4\n * Returns 0 when no number is found.\n */\nexport function modelVersionScore(id) {\n\tvar matches = (id || \"\").match(/\\d+(?:\\.\\d+)?/g);\n\tif (!matches) return 0;\n\tvar max = 0;\n\tfor (var m of matches) {\n\t\tvar v = Number.parseFloat(m);\n\t\tif (v > max) max = v;\n\t}\n\treturn max;\n}"
}
